# Proposed OEIS comments: A234155 to A235908

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A234155

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A234155>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [9451](../papers/09001-09500/09451-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 16*a(n-1) - 59*a(n-2) - 31*a(n-3) + 118*a(n-4) + 35*a(n-5) - 10*a(n-6).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(113 - 703*x - 299*x^2 + 1539*x^3 + 450*x^4 - 130*x^5) / (1 - 16*x + 59*x^2 + 31*x^3 - 118*x^4 - 35*x^5 + 10*x^6). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 13 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 64 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A234161

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A234161>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [3020](../papers/03001-03500/03020-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrences for columns k = 1 and 2 are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A234164

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A234164>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [335](../papers/00001-00500/00335-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 80 (see link above).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 625 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 80, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A234229

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A234229>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [482](../papers/00001-00500/00482-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 40 (see link above).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 512 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 40, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A234269

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A234269>  
Result obtained: **25 August 2026**  
Paper: [11409](../papers/11001-11500/11409-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture D-finite with recurrence: -(n+1)*(2*n-5)*a(n) -2*n*(n-4)*a(n-1) +4*(n-1)*(2*n-3)*a(n-2) +2*(8*n^2-32*n+29)*a(n-3) +4*(n-2)*(2*n-7)*a(n-4)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Jan 24 2020

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
Not only empirical: the generating function given above is algebraic, and a recurrence with polynomial coefficients is a differential operator applied to it. Reducing that operator leaves a polynomial of degree 3, so the recurrence holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 135 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. ## A235408
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A235408>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [4458](../papers/04001-04500/04458-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 73 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 344 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 73, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A235437>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2853](../papers/02501-03000/02853-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical column recurrences are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A235443

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A235443>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [2847](../papers/02501-03000/02847-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 8*a(n-1) - 8*a(n-2) - 16*a(n-3) + 12*a(n-4) + 14*a(n-5) - a(n-6) - 2*a(n-7).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(29 - 41*x - 58*x^2 + 56*x^3 + 56*x^4 - 5*x^5 - 8*x^6) / ((1 - x - x^2)^2*(1 - 6*x - 3*x^2 + 2*x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 19 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 35 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A235445

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A235445>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [4912](../papers/04501-05000/04912-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 53 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 121 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 53, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A235510

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A235510>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [9858](../papers/09501-10000/09858-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) - 6*a(n-3) + a(n-4) + 2*a(n-5).
> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Mar 19 2018: (Start)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [2309](../papers/02001-02500/02309-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 9*a(n-1) -19*a(n-2) -47*a(n-3) +211*a(n-4) -45*a(n-5) -665*a(n-6) +643*a(n-7) +714*a(n-8) -1200*a(n-9) +16*a(n-10) +640*a(n-11) -256*a(n-12)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 49 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 11. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A235769

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A235769>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [4340](../papers/04001-04500/04340-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 88 (see link above).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1202 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 88, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A235843

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A235843>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [9896](../papers/09501-10000/09896-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 14*a(n-1) - 31*a(n-2) - 94*a(n-3) + 192*a(n-4) + 192*a(n-5).
> Empirical g.f.: 4*x*(39 - 150*x - 278*x^2 + 848*x^3 + 768*x^4) / (1 - 14*x + 31*x^2 + 94*x^3 - 192*x^4 - 192*x^5). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 19 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition on a subblock depends only on which values the block holds and how many times, not on where in the block they sit, so it is decided by the few consecutive lines the block spans. Carrying those lines as the state makes the admissible arrays the walks of a finite digraph with 6 vertices, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Paper: [10501](../papers/10501-11000/10501-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 2*a(n-1) + 20*a(n-2) - 8*a(n-3).
> Empirical g.f.: 4*x*(12 + 41*x - 18*x^2) / (1 - 2*x - 20*x^2 + 8*x^3). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 20 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 9 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
